# GetHabit - Easy Habit Tracker (App Store ID 1541469491) — Full Review Analysis

**Corpus:** all **490** text reviews in `App Store Reviews/88. GetHabit - Easy Habit Tracker - Habits Tracker for a BetterMe/reviews.jsonl`
**Window:** 2020-11-25 → 2025-06-14 (1,662 days; 481 of the 490 reviews fall in 2020-11-25 → 2021-12-31) · **Storefronts:** 63 · **Languages:** 10 · **Corpus mean rating: 1.714★**
**Developer:** Fire Arc Technologies Ltd (FireArc Ltd., Herzliya, Israel; bundle `fl.productivity.app`) · **Released:** 2020-11-24 · **Current version:** 1.3.1 (2024-03-26) · **Name in 2020–2021 reviews:** HabitBox
**Method:** every review read individually in its original language; 90 theme codes across 13 families (86 hand-assigned, 4 mechanical) and 23 composite unions; 2,376 code assignments; 64 integrity checks; 20 absence tests; external evidence captured 13 September 2026.

---

## How to read this

Every quantified claim carries a **count, a percentage, the denominator, the market scope, the time window, a signal label and exact review IDs**. Review IDs are `review_id` values from `reviews.jsonl`; the bracketed `#n` is the working index (date order) used by Part 10, so any number resolves to specific reviews and any review resolves to the findings it supports. §9.J lists every review ID behind every code.

Theme counts are **non-exclusive** — a review that describes a double charge and also calls the app a scam is counted in both. Composite unions (`U:` prefix) count each review **once**. Unless a sentence names a narrower scope, the denominator is **490 reviews, all 63 storefronts, 2020-11-25 → 2025-06-14**. Claims written as `` `CODE` = n (x%) `` always use that denominator; segment rates are written "n of N".

### ⚠️ Nine warnings before any number here is used

1. **Text reviewers are a negatively selected minority.** The same 63 storefronts carry **20,597 public ratings** averaging **4.355★**; this corpus is **2.38%** of that base and averages **1.714★**, a gap of **+2.641★**. In the US, 1★ is 448 of 6,623 (6.76%) public ratings but 156 of 203 (76.85%) text reviews. **This corpus describes what made people write, not how the rating base scores the app** — and §0.7 gives reasons to doubt the rating base itself.
2. **Only two storefronts reach the 50-review threshold:** the United States (203) and the United Kingdom (52), together 255 of 490 reviews (52.04%). The other 61 storefronts hold 235 reviews; 25 storefronts have exactly one review. Nothing said about them is more than limited evidence (§6.6).
3. **This is a 2020–2021 corpus.** 481 of the 490 reviews (98.16%) are dated 2020-11-25 → 2021-12-31; nine come after. The findings describe the app as it was sold then — mostly under the name HabitBox — not necessarily as it is today. The listing's last update is version 1.3.1, dated 2024-03-26.
4. **Reviews carry no version field**, and no dated release history could be captured. Every link between a change and what reviewers say is **timing, not proven cause**.
5. **Purchase evidence is what reviewers say.** 182 say they chose to pay (`PAID_EXPLICIT`; a refund request is counted as purchase evidence, §9.A). A further 45 say money was taken without saying they chose a purchase (`CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN`). Neither is a conversion or refund rate.
6. **Prices quoted by reviewers mix plans, currencies and channels** — weekly, monthly, yearly, "lifetime", a paid trial week, content packs, and web checkout. §9.G lists every one as written.
7. **Most of the corpus is English.** 50 of 490 reviews (10.20%) are in nine other languages, all read in the original; translations are the analyst's own and are labelled.
8. **82 reviews (16.73%) have under 60 characters of body text, and 132 (26.94%) carry helpful votes** in a pattern that is itself a finding (§0.7). §9.D re-runs the headline shares without each group.
9. **13 reviews say the app made them rate it before unlocking content** (§3.5), and three carry a star rating that contradicts their text (§4.7). All are kept at the star the reviewer gave.

---

# PART 0 — READ THIS FIRST

## 0.1 Money is the subject of nearly half the corpus

**`U:money_taken` = 227 (46.33%, CI 42.0–50.8%, mean 1.15★, High-priority signal)** — reviews that say the reviewer paid or was charged. `PAID_EXPLICIT` = 182 (37.14%) chose to pay; `CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN` = 45 (9.18%) say money was taken without their choosing.

| Code / union | n | % of 490 | What it records |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `U:paywall_any` | 236 | 48.16% | a wall, price, nag, countdown offer or locked content |
| `U:money_taken` | 227 | 46.33% | reviewer paid or was charged |
| `U:trust_breach` | 197 | 40.20% | scam language, misleading ad or "free" label, distrust of reviews, unauthorised or disguised charges |
| `U:pre_use_wall` | 132 | 26.94% | could not see or try the app before paying |
| `U:paid_problem` | 107 | 21.84% | paid, then no access, double or wrong charge, or more to pay |
| `SCAM_LANGUAGE` | 105 | 21.43% | "scam", "fraud", "Abzocke", "truffa", "estafa" |
| `U:unwanted_charge` | 93 | 18.98% | a charge the reviewer did not expect or agree to |

**It explains the one-star band: 290 of 355 one-star reviews (81.69%) describe paying before they could see the app, or money taken.** Of the 182 reviewers who chose to pay, the mean rating is 1.18★ and 161 gave one star.

- `6832389139` (#134, 2021-01-05, us, 1★) — "It was an additional fee! I’m out $60 and there are 16 challenges... total."
- `7185140969` (#315, 2021-04-05, us, 1★) — "it wants more money for every little thing it is worthless"
- `7200379204` (#344, 2021-04-09, us, 1★) — "Then the first thing I chose, they want $9.99 to unlock the PDFs."

## 0.2 The purchase flow charges people twice

- **`BILL_DOUBLE_CHARGE` = 26 (5.31%, CI 3.6–7.7%)** — charged twice for the same or overlapping purchase. **13 of the 26 also describe the discounted lifetime offer** that appears during or straight after a purchase.
- **`PAY_COUNTDOWN_OFFER` = 46 (9.39%)** — a half-price lifetime offer, usually on a timer.
- **`BILL_WRONG_PLAN` = 15 (3.06%)** — bought lifetime or yearly, then billed weekly or monthly.

The sequence reviewers describe is consistent: buy a plan, see a half-price offer with a countdown, accept it believing it replaces the first, and pay both.

- `6778267872` (#25, 2020-12-21, vn, 1★) — "Then a pop-up showed that I get a 50%off for this deal if I subscribe within one hour. So I did."
- `7029621975` (#221, 2021-02-23, us, 1★) — "Right after I clicked purchase, a pop up comes up claiming to be offering it to you for half off, so $19.99." … "It was simply a double charge."
- `6893430322` (#178, 2021-01-20, us, 1★) — "my subscription says weekly for 7.99 still"

## 0.3 People had to pay before they could see what they were buying

- **`U:pre_use_wall` = 132 (26.94%, CI 23.2–31.0%, mean 1.17★)** — `PAY_HARD_WALL` = 96 (19.59%), `PAY_NO_FREE_TRIAL` = 58 (11.84%), `PAY_FREE_LABEL_MISLEADING` = 33 (6.73%).
- **97 of the 132 did not pay** — these are lost installs, not refund cases.
- **`PAY_PREMIUM_INCOMPLETE` = 38 (7.76%)** — having paid, content was still locked or cost more (PDFs, checklists, challenges).

`6810306576` (#79, 2020-12-30, us, 1★) — "Free to download and the only thing you can do is write your name before purchasing a hefty $8 per week membership plan". `7082241504` (#254, 2021-03-09, us, 1★) — "immediately when opening it and putting in a user name it asked you to pay $39.99".

**The current store description still says "Our app is free to use"** and lists Monthly $4.99, Quarterly $9.99 and Lifetime $19.99; the same listing's in-app purchase list shows "Premium Access" at $7.99, $9.99, $19.99, $29.99 and $39.99 [external, §2.2]. Two reviewers said in 2021 that in-app prices were double the listing's (`6832829686`, `6934056192`).

## 0.4 The ad was not the app

- **`U:ad_mismatch` = 104 (21.22%, CI 17.8–25.1%)** — `ADS_MISLEADING` = 72 (14.69%) say the app is not what the ad showed; the rest object to the "free" label.
- **`ACQ_SOCIAL_AD` = 53 (10.82%)** came from Facebook, Instagram, TikTok or tumblr.
- **52 of the 72 misleading-ad reviewers paid.** The ad did its job; the product did not match it.
- **`USE_CLEANING` = 12 (2.45%)** wanted the cleaning checklist they had seen advertised — 11 of them in the US.

`7084713878` (#256, 2021-03-09, us, 1★) — "the ad showed this super detailed all inclusive checklist for cleaning" … "Then I go looking for the checklist that was advertised on fb and can’t find it anywhere." `6715202713` (#2, 2020-12-04, jp, 1★) — "The Instagram add suggested that it had something to do with personality types".

## 0.5 In mid-2021 the complaint moved from the paywall to the card statement

From June 2021 reviewers describe buying or trialling **through a website reached from Instagram or Facebook**, then being billed outside the App Store.

| Measure | E1 launch (n=192) | E2 spring 2021 (n=201) | E3 Jun–Dec 2021 (n=88) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `U:pre_use_wall` | 55 (28.6%) | 70 (34.8%) | 7 (8.0%) |
| `U:unwanted_charge` | 22 (11.5%) | 27 (13.4%) | **42 (47.7%)** |
| `CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN` | 2 (1.0%) | 6 (3.0%) | **35 (39.8%)** |
| `BILL_WEB_CHECKOUT` | 0 | 0 | **18 (20.5%)** |
| `BILL_CANT_CANCEL` | 5 (2.6%) | 3 (1.5%) | **22 (25.0%)** |

`BILL_WEB_CHECKOUT` first appears on 2021-06-03. `BILL_OTHER_MERCHANT_NAME` = 4 (0.82%), all in August 2021: `7692398130` (#445, tr) — "with the name PersonalityPath (they impersonate this company)"; `7674187696` (#436, gb) — "using another companys name on bank statements". `7621555490` (#427, gb) — "purchased the free trial week via their website" … "cannot use the premium offer, it’s asking me to pay again".

## 0.6 Support did not answer, and refunds were rare

- **`SUPPORT_UNRESPONSIVE` = 59 (12.04%)**; `U:support_negative` = 70 (14.29%).
- **`BILL_REFUND_REQUEST` = 64 (13.06%)**; **`BILL_REFUND_RECEIVED` = 4 (0.82%)**; `BILL_REFUND_REFUSED` = 7 (1.43%).
- **61 of the 227 money-taken reviewers (26.9%) also report a support failure** (segment rate).

A support reply quoted by `7932137213` (#470, 2021-10-19, us, 1★): "we are unable to monitor every double-subscription case since there is a huge number of such mistakes." Two Spanish reviewers were refunded quickly (`7879858284`, `8036849766`, §3.8).

## 0.7 The public rating does not look like the reviews, and the corpus shows why it might not

- **A review gate.** `REVIEW_GATE` = 13 (2.65%) say the app made them rate it before unlocking a challenge; 8 of the 13 had not yet used the app. `6824431033` (#104, us, 3★) — "The app makes you rate it before unlocking a challenge." Prompting ratings in exchange for content would push the public rating up regardless of experience.
- **Helpful votes run backwards.** 27 of 54 five-star reviews (50%) carry helpful votes, against 80 of 355 one-star reviews (23%); five-star reviews average **2.74** votes, one-star reviews **0.37**. **11 five-star US reviews with under 60 characters carry five or more votes** ("Cool", 9 votes; "Great app", 12 votes).
- **Two reviewers allege fake reviews** — `6794221347` (#46) "Identical reviews with two different usernames" — and `REVIEWS_DISTRUST` = 11 (2.24%). The corpus contains **no repeated review body**, so the allegation cannot be confirmed here.

**None of this proves manipulation.** It is enough that the 4.355★ public rating should not be used as evidence of satisfaction for this app (§9.H).

## 0.8 What anyone liked

- **`U:praise_any` = 90 (18.37%)**, of which **`U:praise_specific` = 33 (6.73%)** name a quality; `OUT_BENEFIT` = 20 (4.08%) say it helped.
- **Only 53 reviews (10.82%) praise without any complaint** (mean 4.77★); 35 of the 54 five-star reviews are under 60 characters.
- **Of 182 buyers, one is satisfied** (`PAID_SATISFIED`, `7391062590`, 3★): "I purchased the app and it has helped with daily/weekly/monthly habits and to-do lists."
- **The idea is liked more than the app.** `PRAISE_CONCEPT` = 15 (3.06%, mean 1.53★): `7067923282` "the concept is amazing but of course you have to buy everything for it to work".

## 0.9 What this corpus shows is *absent*

Tested against all 490 reviews with multilingual patterns; every hit was read individually (§9.F). **No Apple Watch, battery, Family Sharing, sleep-tracking or in-app AI mention.** One crash report (`7010825409`), one broken widget (`7549137355`), one Apple Health mention (`6823254779`). **No developer reply to a review is quoted except one reviewer who saw a response months later** (`8034491173`).

# PART 1 — SCOPE AND METHOD

## 1.1 Files used

| Path | Role |
|---|---|
| `App Store Reviews/88. …/reviews.jsonl` | **The corpus.** 490 records, one JSON object per line. |
| `App Store Reviews/88. …/by_country/*.jsonl` | 63 per-storefront files, 490 records total. |
| `App Store Reviews/88. …/manifest.json` | Extraction metadata (2026-09-08): 490 reviews, 63 storefronts, rating distribution 54/13/27/41/355, mean 1.714. |
| `App Store Reviews/88. …/_state.json` | Crawl state for **157 storefronts**: 63 with reviews, 94 swept and empty, 0 incomplete. |

The source folder is read-only; nothing was written to it. All working artefacts are in `Temp/` (`88-*`).

## 1.2 Schema actually present

13 fields, populated on all 490 records, with no nulls and no empty strings:

| Field | Notes |
|---|---|
| `review_id` | Primary key. **490 unique, no duplicates.** 10 digits (488) or 11 digits (2). |
| `app_id` / `app_name` | 1541469491 / `GetHabit - Easy Habit Tracker` throughout. Seven reviews call the app **HabitBox** in their text (mechanical code `META_HABITBOX_NAME`, 2020-11-25 → 2021-04-15). |
| `country` / `country_name` | 63 storefronts. **Storefront, not language** (§6.7). |
| `rating` | 1–5, all five values present. |
| `title` | "Scam" occurs 12 times; "Not free" 7; "Rip off" and "Don’t do it" 5 each; "Abzocke" and "Disappointing" 4 each. No title+body pair repeats. |
| `body` | All 490 distinct. Median length 165.5 characters. |
| `author` | All 490 distinct. **No repeat authors.** |
| `date` | ISO-8601 UTC. Up to 15 reviews on one calendar day (§9.H). |
| `vote_count` / `vote_sum` | 132 reviews carry helpful votes. |
| `is_edited` | 4 reviews edited (`7793704069`, `7887870313`, `8034491173`, `8103231344`). |

**There is no `version` field.**

## 1.3 Coverage and reconciliation

Run by `88-reconcile.py`; output in `Temp/88-reconcile-out.txt`. Every check is clean.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Records read from `reviews.jsonl` | **490 — 100% of the corpus** |
| Unique `review_id` | 490 (**0 duplicates**) |
| Records across the 63 `by_country` files | 490; ID set identical to the main file |
| Country records not identical, field by field, to the main-file record | **0** |
| Country field ≠ file name | **0** |
| `manifest.json` total / per-country counts / storefront count / rating distribution | **all match exactly** |
| Mean rating recomputed vs manifest | 1.714 vs 1.714 ✓ |
| `_state.json` collected counts vs main file | **0 differences**; 157 storefronts swept, 0 incomplete |
| Exact title+body duplicates / exact body duplicates | **0 / 0** |
| Repeat author names | **0** |

**No deduplication was necessary or performed.**

## 1.4 Processing method

1. `88-dump.py` — parse all 490 records, HTML-unescape, sort by `(date, review_id)`, emit `88-all-reviews.txt` (every review in full) and `88-index.tsv`.
2. **Every review read individually, in full, in its original language, in date order.** No sampling, no keyword filtering, no clustering. The 50 non-English reviews (Spanish 17, German 13, Italian 7, Portuguese 6, French 3, Norwegian, Czech, Russian and Dutch 1 each) were read in the original.
3. `88-themes.py` — a 90-code, 13-family taxonomy built **from** the corpus after reading it, with a definition and direction for every code, plus 23 unions. **Every code is used.**
4. `88-cls/001-070.txt` … `421-490.txt` — the hand classification, seven files: one line per review with the working index, the `review_id`, its codes, its language and a note quoting the exact words that drove each code (with translations). **This is the expensive artefact.**
5. `88-build-classification.py` — asserts every line's `review_id` against the index rebuilt from `reviews.jsonl`, refuses unknown codes, duplicate lines, unclassified reviews or hand-assigned mechanical codes, then emits `88-review-classification.py` (CLASSIFICATION, THEME_TO_IDS, LANG, META, NOTES). `META_LOW_INFO`, `META_EDITED`, `META_HAS_VOTES` and `META_HABITBOX_NAME` are assigned **mechanically** from the source record.
6. `88-validate.py` — **64 checks, all passing**: 10 structural (zero unknown IDs, zero intra-theme duplicates, zero unassigned records, every code used), 4 mechanical, 3 language, 36 that tie codes to the literal review text, 11 consistency rules. **The first run caught two real classification errors** (§9.C).
7. `88-aggregate.py` — every count, union, star band, storefront, eligible-country table, market group, era, month, money-taken segment, price point, sensitivity run, vote and burst check, and absence test; writes `88-gen/`.
8. `88-build-report.py` assembles this report; `88-verify-report.py` re-derives its claims afterwards (§9.M).

## 1.5 Signal thresholds

Applied to the global corpus (denominator 490) and separately to the US (203) and the UK (52) with their own denominators.

| Share | Label |
|---|---|
| <0.1% | Ignore by default |
| 0.1–<0.5% | Weak signal |
| 0.5–<1% | Emerging signal |
| 1–<3% | Meaningful signal |
| 3–5% | Very strong signal |
| >5% | High-priority signal |

**A caution on this scale at n = 490.** One review is 0.20% (Weak), three are 0.61% (Emerging), five are 1.02% (Meaningful) and 25 are 5.10% (High-priority). In the UK a single review is already 1.9%. Read the count and the confidence interval, not only the label, for anything under ten.

## 1.6 Limitations, stated plainly

1. **Negative selection** (warning 1). The corpus over-represents people with a grievance, and here most grievances are about money.
2. **Two eligible countries.** Everything else is limited evidence.
3. **A 2020–2021 corpus.** Nine reviews after 2021; nothing here describes the current build.
4. **No version field, no release history, no archived listing.** Wayback holds no capture of this listing (§9.E). Eras are defined from the corpus.
5. **Purchases and charges are self-reported.** No conversion, refund or chargeback rate can be derived. Some charge complaints may describe the reviewer's own mistake, and several reviewers say so ("my own fault", "my bad").
6. **The app was not tested**, and the 2021 web checkout could not be seen.
7. **Translations are the analyst's own.**
8. **Non-exclusive counts.** Codes overlap by design; unions give once-per-review counts.

# PART 2 — PRODUCT AND MONETIZATION MODEL

## 2.1 What the product is, from the reviews

In 2020–2021 reviewers describe **HabitBox**: an onboarding that asks for a name and goals, **preset challenges** (7- to 30-day checklists such as cleaning, mental health or minimalism), **custom habits with reminders**, **task lists**, a few **articles**, **statistics**, and **printable PDF checklists sold separately**. `6756282701` (#10, us) — "What do you want to work on? It asks you. Reading? Drinking water? DO SPORT????"

| Feature | Evidence in reviews | Representative IDs |
|---|---|---|
| Preset challenges | `PRAISE_CHALLENGES` 7; `CONTENT_THIN` 54; `CONTENT_LOW_QUALITY` 19 | `6772040713`, `7111600945`, `7045242022` ("Minimalism challenge. Day 1 Purge your wardrobe."), `6859899210` |
| Custom habits and reminders | `DEF_REMINDERS` 18; `FUNC_LIMITED` 13 | `6809718942`, `7287583575`, `6839825405` |
| Task lists and planner | `PRAISE_ORGANISER` 4; lists or tasks disappearing `DEF_DATA_LOST` 2 | `7391062590`, `6823277588`, `6918939563`, `7088772298` |
| Articles | "just 5 articles" | `6860262565` |
| Printable PDFs / checklists | `PAY_PREMIUM_INCOMPLETE` 38 | `7200379204`, `7084713878`, `7142740797` |
| Statistics / progress | `DEF_CANT_TRACK` 7 | `6860262565` ("statistics is broken"), `7184164608` |
| Account, sync, backup | none; `NO_ACCOUNT_SYNC` 9 | `6823254779`, `7010825409` |
| Languages | `LANG_NOT_AVAILABLE` 6 | `6826530656` ("Comprei o aplicativo e ele está todo em alemão" *[bought the app and it's all in German]*), `8036849766` |
| Ads in the free version | `ADS_IN_APP` 21 | `6781000648`, `7189294257` |
| Review prompt tied to content | `REVIEW_GATE` 13 | `6804556598`, `7261488968` |

## 2.3 Free / paid / trial / unclear — classified

Prices are **as quoted by reviewers** in 2020–2021, in their currency (§9.G).

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Download | **Free** | Listing |
| Using the app at all, Dec 2020 – Apr 2021 | **Paid, per most reviewers.** A purchase screen after the name; `PAY_HARD_WALL` 96. Five reviewers got past it or used parts free (`FREE_TIER_OK`, e.g. `6930069664` "if you wait 5 or so seconds an ‘X’ pops up") | `PAY_HARD_WALL` 96, `FREE_TIER_OK` 5 |
| Trial | **Paid or card-gated.** A $0.49 / £0.49 / 0,49€ first week, or three days free with card details; in the web checkout a "$1 verification of funds" (`7637881316`), €0.01 (`7974564520`) or $0.99 (`7611454280`) | `PAY_PAID_TRIAL` 29 |
| Weekly plan | **Paid**, $7.99 / £7.99 / 69 kr; reviewers expecting lifetime or yearly were billed weekly | `PAY_WEEKLY_PLAN` 20, `BILL_WRONG_PLAN` 15 |
| Monthly / 3-month / yearly | **Paid**, $9.99 / £9.99 a month; $30 for three months (`7199743576`); $40 a year | §9.G |
| Lifetime | **One-time**, $39.99 / £38.99, offered at $19.99 / £19.99 / 21,99 € with a countdown | `PAY_COUNTDOWN_OFFER` 46 |
| Content inside a paid plan | **Extra-paid, per 38 reviewers**: PDFs and checklists $9.99; a "Full-Time-Pass" 10,99€ (`7188581081`); "an additional $16 per month to access further content" (`6894642285`) | `PAY_PREMIUM_INCOMPLETE` 38; listing sells printable items separately at $9.99 [external] |
| Web checkout (from June 2021) | **Paid outside the App Store**, by card or PayPal, about $31.96 / 31,98€; plans not visible in Apple's subscription list | `BILL_WEB_CHECKOUT` 19, `BILL_CANT_CANCEL` 34 |
| Ad-promised trial length | **Unclear.** Reviewers cite ads offering a week, 28 days, 30 days or a month, then a charge within days | `BILL_TRIAL_SHORTER` 8 |
| Today | **Unclear.** The listing says "free to use" with monthly, quarterly and lifetime plans; the in-app purchase list still carries $7.99 and $39.99 items | Listing |

## 2.4 Data and privacy statements

| Source | What it says |
|---|---|
| App Store privacy label | **Data Used to Track You**; **Data Linked to You**, including for the developer's advertising or marketing |
| Privacy policy (2022-12-20) | payment processors PayPal, Solid, Apple Pay, Google Pay; FireArc Ltd. is the data controller |
| Subscription terms (2022-12-18) | cancel through Apple Pay or Google Pay; fees non-refundable; trials auto-renew unless cancelled 24 hours before the end |

**Eight reviewers object to handing over payment details to try the app** (`PRIV_PAYMENT_DETAILS` = 8 (1.63%)): `7142966385` (#287) "It’s not actually free trial when you force ppl to put their credit card data!!"; `7672761451` (#434) "Don’t use their trial with your card details. This is a trap!" No review raises tracking or data sharing.

**The 2022 terms describe cancellation through Apple or Google only; 2021 reviewers who bought on the website say that route did not exist for them** (§3.3). The terms post-date those reviews, so this is not evidence of what was promised in 2021.

## 2.5 What could not be established

- **Downloads, conversion, revenue, refund and chargeback rates.**
- **The 2021 web checkout** — its pages, trial terms and plan names — and **whether "PersonalityPath" is a related entity** or a billing descriptor.
- **When the app was renamed** from HabitBox to GetHabit; the app ID is the same throughout.
- **Whether the review gate, the countdown offer and the paid trial still exist.**
- **Why the store description's prices ($4.99 / $9.99 / $19.99) differ from the in-app purchase list ($7.99 – $39.99).**

# PART 3 — GLOBAL FINDINGS

All denominators are 490 reviews, all 63 storefronts, 2020-11-25 → 2025-06-14, unless stated. Counts are non-exclusive; unions count each review once.

## 3.1 The prioritised picture

| Union | n | % of 490 | CI 95% | Mean ★ | Signal |

Five findings carry the corpus:

1. **Money** — `U:money_taken` = 227 (46.33%) paid or were charged, and `U:paid_problem` = 107 (21.84%) hit a problem after paying (§3.3, §5).
2. **Paying before seeing** — `U:pre_use_wall` = 132 (26.94%), and a store label that said "free" (§3.2).
3. **Trust** — `U:trust_breach` = 197 (40.20%): scam language, misleading ads, disguised or unauthorised charges (§3.5, §3.9).
4. **Charges people did not agree to** — `U:unwanted_charge` = 93 (18.98%), concentrated from June 2021 (§3.3, §7).
5. **A thin product** — `U:value_content` = 105 (21.43%) say there was little inside, or nothing a free list couldn't do (§3.6).

`U:negative_any` = 429 (87.55%). Praise of any kind is `U:praise_any` = 90 (18.37%).

**A purchase screen stood between install and use.** `PAY_HARD_WALL` = 96 (19.59%, mean 1.15★). `6802711687` (#61, us) — "It asks for your name, then tries to sell you a subscription." `7169002761` (#297, us) — "you can’t get past the subscription page without making a selection. There is no free version."

**The "free" label made it worse.** `PAY_FREE_LABEL_MISLEADING` = 33 (6.73%, mean 1.06★). `6812397351` (#80, gb) — "Says free to use and can subscribe and pay, which is wrong." `6824404281` (#103, ar) — "me parece de mala fe ponerla como Gratis en el Store" *[listing it as Free in the store seems bad faith to me]*.

**No trial, or a trial that costs money.** `PAY_NO_FREE_TRIAL` = 58 (11.84%); `PAY_PAID_TRIAL` = 29 (5.92%). `7092708463` (#269, us) — "Was met with a $0.49 charge to even enter the app". The weekly plan drew particular anger: `PAY_WEEKLY_PLAN` = 20 (4.08%) — `6771958876` (#15, dk) "3 days for free, then they’ll charge 69 kroners (about 10$) per WEEK!!"; `6756282701` (#10, us) "charge EIGHT DOLLARS A WEEK".

**Paying did not unlock everything.** `PAY_PREMIUM_INCOMPLETE` = 38 (7.76%, mean 1.16★) — 30 of them from reviewers who chose to pay. `7227200950` (#362, ca) — "Not worth 3 different life time membership fees I had to pay". `7188581081` (#324, de) — "für 10,99€ noch zusätzlich zu erwerben, halte ich wirklich für sehr übertrieben" *[buying the "Full-Time-Pass" for 10.99€ on top I find really excessive]*.

**Pressure.** `PAY_UPSELL_NAGS` = 36 (7.35%) and `PAY_COUNTDOWN_OFFER` = 46 (9.39%). `6819531238` (#93, us) — "I've seen 4 attempted at making me buy upgrades in the first 1 minute."

**Price itself.** `PAY_PRICE_TOO_HIGH` = 54 (11.02%, mean 1.52★) — usually against how little the app contained, not against a competitor's price. `6743042296` (#5, gb) — "Insane amount to charge."

**Would-be buyers name their conditions.** `PAY_CONDITIONAL_WILLING` = 8 (1.63%): a real trial (`6818850233` "A successful 30 day trial and I probably would have paid the subscription"; `6827257273` "If I had a 14 or even 7 day trial I would have actually given it ago."), sync (`6852598066` "Want my money? Add cross device syncing."), a lower price or honest advertising (`6793208836`, `6743042296`).

**Double charges came from the offer.** `BILL_DOUBLE_CHARGE` = 26 (5.31%); 13 also describe the half-price offer (§0.2). `BILL_PRICE_MISMATCH` = 10 (2.04%) were charged more than the price shown — `7090478565` (#268, us) "Said it was charging me 39.99. I was charged 75 freaking dollars."

**The wrong plan.** `BILL_WRONG_PLAN` = 15 (3.06%). `6867290877` (#169, ca) — "I purchased a year subscription on this app but was still charged the weekly rate after my trial."

**Trials that billed early.** `BILL_TRIAL_CONVERSION` = 35 (7.14%), 25 of them in June–December 2021; `BILL_TRIAL_SHORTER` = 8 (1.63%), all in that window. `7637881316` (#432, us) — "they offer you a 3 day subscription but charge you $31 immediately." `7855046339` (#461, es) — "He aceptado la prueba que ponía que era de UN MES y automáticamente me han cobrado 20€" *[I accepted the trial that said ONE MONTH and was automatically charged 20€]*.

**Cancelling did not stop charges.** `BILL_CHARGED_AFTER_CANCEL` = 21 (4.29%); `BILL_CANT_CANCEL` = 34 (6.94%); `BILL_UNAUTHORISED` = 17 (3.47%). `7058565193` (#235, us) — "I sent three emails asking to cancel that night and one the following day." `7793704069` (#459, ru) — "Подключил их подписку на сайте и теперь не могу отключить!" *[subscribed on their website and now can't cancel]*. `8776444875` (#487, mx) — "ya van meses que me realizan el cobro" *[they've been charging me for months]*.

**Web checkout and another merchant's name.** `BILL_WEB_CHECKOUT` = 19 (3.88%) and `BILL_OTHER_MERCHANT_NAME` = 4 (0.82%), all from June 2021 onward (§0.5). 8 of the 19 web buyers could not get the purchase into the app (`PAID_NO_ACCESS`).

**Refunds.** `BILL_REFUND_REQUEST` = 64 (13.06%). `BILL_REFUND_REFUSED` = 7 (1.43%): `6998518011` (#209, us) "tried to request a refund TWICE from Apple and was denied"; `7862429664` (#462, ca) "I was told I had to pruebe I used the app at least during 7 days". `BILL_REFUND_RECEIVED` = 4 (0.82%): `6838351982`, `7879858284`, `8036849766`, `8103231344`. `BILL_BANK_ACTION` = 8 (1.63%) went to their bank or blocked their card.

- **Misleading ads.** `ADS_MISLEADING` = 72 (14.69%, mean 1.14★) — personality types, cleaning checklists, templates and challenges shown in Facebook, Instagram, TikTok and tumblr ads that the app did not contain (§0.4).
- **Ads inside the app.** `ADS_IN_APP` = 21 (4.29%), all before 2021-04-07. `6781000648` (#31, pl) — "Impossible to use because of enormous number of long video ads."
- **Rate to unlock.** `REVIEW_GATE` = 13 (2.65%). `6830128790` (#127, us) — "it forced me to leave a review to unlock the first habit tracker". `7261488968` (#377, us) — "writing a review in exchange for trying the app".
- **Distrust of the rating.** `REVIEWS_DISTRUST` = 11 (2.24%). `7029621975` (#221) — "I’m assuming those are paid reviews"; `7257033437` (#376) — "I was a fool for trusting the 4.4 star rating."; `7441400045` (#398, no) says the developer chose to "delete the previous review".

**`U:value_content` = 105 (21.43%, mean 1.33★).**

- **Little inside.** `CONTENT_THIN` = 54 (11.02%). `6818382575` (#89, us) — "There are like 5 challenges and they are laughable."
- **Low-quality content.** `CONTENT_LOW_QUALITY` = 19 (3.88%). `7079611283` (#252, us) — "the cleaning list is like Day 1 do the dishes, day 2 make the bed."
- **Not for me.** `CONTENT_NOT_PERSONAL` = 13 (2.65%). `6775201953` (#21, au) — "a number of the challenges appear to be aimed females and/or those in a relationship".
- **Basic habit functions missing.** `FUNC_LIMITED` = 13 (2.65%). `6839825405` (#149, mx) — "you can’t change the name of a habit, you can’t add more habit categories, you cant change the order of habits within a category". `7569539286` (#413, ca) — counting habits ("8 glasses of water") cannot be tracked.
- **Free substitutes.** `COMP_FREE_ALTERNATIVE` = 33 (6.73%, mean 1.33★): the phone's reminders and notes, a journal, Pinterest and Google lists. `COMP_NAMED_APP` = 7 (1.43%): Streaks, Fabulous, Moodlinks, HabitMinder, Habit RPG, Any.do, Outlook.

# PART 4 — RATINGS ANALYSIS

## 4.1 The distribution

| Stars | n | % of 490 | Wilson 95% CI |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 5★ | 54 | 11.02% | 8.5–14.1% |
| 4★ | 13 | 2.65% | 1.6–4.5% |
| 3★ | 27 | 5.51% | 3.8–7.9% |
| 2★ | 41 | 8.37% | 6.2–11.2% |
| 1★ | 355 | 72.45% | 68.3–76.2% |

**Mean 1.714★, median 1★.** 355 of 490 reviews (72.45%) are one-star; 54 (11.02%) are five-star; 81 sit in between. The public rating across the same storefronts is 4.355★ (§6.8).

**The bands differ in kind, not degree.** Money taken rises from 2% of five-star to 57% of one-star reviews; praise falls from 89% to 4%. Five-star reviews are short (65% under 60 characters) and half carry helpful votes. Four-star reviews are the requests band (38%). The review gate is most visible in the middle bands: 3 of 27 three-star and 3 of 41 two-star reviews describe it.

## 4.3 Five-star reviews (54, 11.02%)

- **48 of 54 praise the app (89%)**, but **29 of those name no quality** (`PRAISE_GENERIC`) and **35 of the 54 are under 60 characters** ("Cool", "Awesome", "Good").
- **27 of 54 carry helpful votes** — 50%, against 23% of one-star reviews (§0.7).
- **5 have not used the app yet** (`META_NOT_YET_USED`): `6822135295` (#96) "Excited to try this in the new year!"
- **One five-star reviewer paid** — and warns against the app: `7269202665` (#382, us) "the reviews tend to show to 5 star reviews first so that’s why I gave it 5 stars. don’t download this app."
- The specific five-star praise is about outcomes and design: `6772040713` (#16, gb) "As a person who prefers keeping everything in one organized and accessible place, this is my GO-TO on a daily!"

## 4.4 Four-star reviews (13, 2.65%)

*Good, and please add…* — 11 of 13 praise the app and 5 carry a request (38%): more challenges (`6803331432`, `6830719044`, `6839806124`), editable challenges (`6825413129`), theme colours (`6822461462`). One is a review-gate complaint given four stars: `7240052353` (#367) "Why its just a challenge u just want clout smh...".

## 4.5 Three-star reviews (27, 5.51%)

**Mixed and billing-heavy.** 10 of 27 carry a paywall code and 9 report money taken; 8 of the 9 chose to pay. Three describe the review gate and one says only that: `7069536244` (#244, us) "Just needed to rate to unlock". The one satisfied buyer in the corpus is here (`7391062590`), as is a refunded Spanish reviewer (`8036849766`).

## 4.6 Two- and one-star reviews (396, 80.82%)

- **Two-star (41):** 20 paywall (49%), 13 money taken (32%), **12 defects (29%)** — the highest defect share of any band: black screens, broken reminders, stuck set-up (`7105962949`, `6809718942`, `7159739863`).
- **One-star (355):** **204 money taken (57%)**, 118 pre-use wall (33%), 104 scam language (29%), 93 ad mismatch (26%); only 13 contain any praise (4%).
- **290 of the 355 one-star reviews (81.69%) describe paying before seeing the app, or money taken.** The other 65 are in-app ads (`6781000648`, `7168616793`), thin or silly content (`7078094120`, `7184180634`), spelling (`7006473743`, `7050664530`), broken UI or tracking (`6830845373`, `7521527537`), the review gate (`6804556598`), price objections without paying (`6774457480`) and bare scam accusations (`7190399865`).

## 4.7 Rating-versus-text contradictions

`META_RATING_TEXT_MISMATCH` = 3 (0.61%). Retained at the star given:

| # | Review ID | Date | Store | ★ | Text |
|---:|---|---|---|---:|---|
| 367 | `7240052353` | 2021-04-19 | us | 4★ | review-gate complaint only |
| 382 | `7269202665` | 2021-04-27 | us | 5★ | "DO NOT DOWNLOAD THIS APP." — double charge; gave five stars to be seen |
| 387 | `7278892888` | 2021-04-29 | us | 5★ | asked for money on every tap; app freezes; asks for help |

Beyond these three, 5 five-star, 2 four-star and 4 three-star reviews say they have not used the app yet, and 13 reviews describe being made to rate it (§3.5). **Some stars in this corpus were given before any experience of the product.**

---

# PART 5 — PAID-USER AND UPGRADE ANALYSIS

## 5.1 The monetisation model

In 2020–2021 reviewers describe **a hard paywall at onboarding** leading to **a paid or card-gated trial**, **weekly, monthly, three-month, yearly and "lifetime" plans**, **a half-price lifetime offer on a timer**, and **content packs (PDFs, checklists) sold on top**. From June 2021, **purchases and trials through a website reached from social ads**, billed by card or PayPal outside the App Store (§2.3).

| Evidence | Finding |
|---|---|
| Reviewers who say they chose to pay | **`PAID_EXPLICIT` = 182 (37.14%)** |
| …of whom report a post-purchase problem | **93 of 182 (51.1%)** (`U:paid_problem`) |
| …of whom regret the purchase | 65 of 182 (35.7%) |
| …of whom ask for a refund | 50 of 182 (27.5%) |
| …of whom are satisfied | **1 of 182** |
| Reviewers charged without saying they chose to buy | **`CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN` = 45 (9.18%)** |
| Refunds reported received | 4 |

**No conversion, refund or retention rate can be derived from reviews.**

**By era.** Chose to pay: 56 in E1, 94 in E2, 27 in E3, 5 in E4. Charged without choosing: 2, 6, **35**, 2. **By storefront.** Chose to pay: US 98, UK 14, Canada 9, Australia 7. Charged without choosing: US 7, Canada 5, Spain 5, Germany 4.

## 5.3 What buyers say they bought

A keyword search over the 182 buyers' reviews — hits counted, not read one by one, so indicative only (non-exclusive; IDs in `88-aggregate-out.txt`): "lifetime" or equivalent in 58, a week or trial week in 41, a year in 17, a month in 15, three months in 1. **Lifetime is the plan buyers name most often.**

## 5.4 What triggers a purchase

| Trigger | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **The ad** | 52 of the 72 `ADS_MISLEADING` reviewers paid. `6817958869` (#88, gb) "Nothing like the Facebook adds. Was so excited to purchase but left sorely disappointed." |
| **The half-price lifetime offer** | 40 of the 46 `PAY_COUNTDOWN_OFFER` reviewers paid. `7088093804` (#263, us) "It’s originally FORTY so I went ahead and got it." |
| **Paying to see the app at all** | `PAY_PAID_TRIAL` 16 buyers. `7062384160` (#238, us) "you have to pay to even access the app. Whatever, did that." |
| **Hope that more content sits behind the next payment** | `7084713878` (#256, us) "Which I also paid thinking maybe I would see more checklists after I paid." |
| **A free trial that billed** | `CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN` 45; 28 of them describe a trial conversion |

Several buyers blame themselves: `6793208836` (#44, us) "which was my own fault for not just trying it first"; `6950304754` (#198, us) "My bad for purchasing the “lifetime” for $40".

## 5.5 What goes wrong after purchase

**Among the 182 who chose to pay:** an unwanted charge 51 (28.0%) — double charge 24 (13.2%), wrong plan 15 (8.2%), price mismatch 8 (4.4%); content still locked 30 (16.5%); no access at all 25 (13.7%); a support failure 43 (23.6%); scam language 50 (27.5%).

**Among the 45 charged without choosing:** a trial that billed 28 (62.2%); charged after cancelling 15 (33.3%); could not cancel 16 (35.6%); unauthorised 13 (28.9%); web checkout 8 (17.8%); support failure 18 (40.0%); bank action 5 (11.1%); refund received 3 (6.7%).

`6883817922` (#172, gb) sets out the buyer's sequence: "I also fell for the £19.99 offer" … "it is NOT telling me I have a lifetime membership but a WEEK membership" … "I would like a refund but seeing as the creator isn’t responding to these reviews, I don’t hold much hope."

## 5.6 Upgrade barriers among non-buyers

| Barrier | n | % of 490 | Representative |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Wall before any use, did not pay | 97 | 19.80% | `6802711687`, `7169002761`, `7195907201` |
| No free trial or preview (`PAY_NO_FREE_TRIAL`) | 58 | 11.84% | `6778875717`, `7194236413`, `7543647307` |
| Price too high (`PAY_PRICE_TOO_HIGH`) | 54 | 11.02% | `6743042296`, `7179374028` |
| Card details for a "free" trial (`PRIV_PAYMENT_DETAILS`) | 8 | 1.63% | `7142966385`, `7202360769` |
| Would pay with a real trial, sync or lower price (`PAY_CONDITIONAL_WILLING`) | 8 | 1.63% | `6818850233`, `6852598066` |

**The barrier is the order of events, not the price alone:** people were asked to pay before seeing anything, and many who paid then found little inside.

## 5.7 What cannot be claimed here

- **No conversion rate.** 182 buyers against 132 pre-use-wall complaints is not a ratio of anything: both are self-selected.
- **No fraud finding.** Reviewers allege fraud; the corpus shows consistent complaints about how purchases were presented and billed. Some reviewers acknowledge their own mistakes.
- **No estimate of refunds paid.** Four reviewers say they were refunded; others may have been without writing.

## 6.2 Eligibility

The threshold is 50 reviews. **The United States (203) and the United Kingdom (52) qualify**; together they are 255 of 490 reviews (52.04%). The next largest are Canada 34, Australia 23, Germany 13 and the Philippines 10; 25 storefronts have exactly one review. All 490 reviews are in every global figure.

**What is distinctive about the US (with counts):**

- **The paywall and the ad.** `U:paywall_any` 114 of 203 (56.2%) vs 42.5% elsewhere; `U:ad_mismatch` 60 of 203 (29.6%) vs 15.3%. `USE_CLEANING` 11 of 203 (5.4%) vs 1 review elsewhere — the cleaning-checklist ad ran here.
- **Buyers, not trial victims.** `PAID_EXPLICIT` 98 of 203 (48.3%) vs 29.3%; `CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN` 7 of 203 (3.4%) vs 13.2%. **Content still locked after paying:** 26 of 203 (12.8%) vs 4.2%; **the countdown offer:** 26 of 203 (12.8%) vs 7.0%.
- **Less web-checkout harm.** `BILL_WEB_CHECKOUT` 2 of 203 (1.0%) vs 5.9%; `BILL_CANT_CANCEL` 7 of 203 (3.4%) vs 9.4%.
- **The review gate and the votes.** `REVIEW_GATE` 9 of 203 (4.4%) vs 1.4%; reviews with helpful votes 68 of 203 (33.5%) vs 22.3%. **18 of the 23 US five-star reviews carry votes.**
- **Public rating 4.35★ over 6,623 ratings; 1★ is 448 of 6,623 (6.76%) publicly and 156 of 203 (76.85%) in text.**

## 6.4 United Kingdom — 52 reviews, mean 2.06★ — eligible

| Union / code | GB n / 52 | GB % | CI 95% | GB signal (own denominator) | Mean ★ | Rest n / 438 | Rest % | Difference |

Signal labels use the UK denominator (52); one review is 1.9%.

**What is distinctive about the UK (with counts):**

- **Fewer buyers, less fraud language.** `U:money_taken` 16 of 52 (30.8%) vs 48.2% elsewhere; `SCAM_LANGUAGE` 6 of 52 (11.5%) vs 22.6%.
- **The weekly plan in pounds.** `PAY_WEEKLY_PLAN` 5 of 52 (9.6%) vs 3.4%: `6827257273` "only gives you a 3 day free trial then £8 per week after".
- **More praise.** `U:praise_any` 13 of 52 (25.0%) vs 17.6%; `U:praise_specific` 6 of 52 (11.5%) vs 6.2%. The mean is the higher of the two eligible storefronts.
- **No cleaning-checklist complaints** (0 of 52).
- **Public rating 4.38★ over 3,203 ratings.**

## 6.5 High-spend markets and high-review-volume markets (both defined)

**High-spend group — defined, not measured.** No spend or download data exists. For comparability with the other reports in this series the same list is used: **us, gb, au, ca, de, ch, fr, it, es, nl, be, at, no, fi, sg, ae**. Fifteen are present (fi is not). **The US is 203 of the group's 372 reviews.**

**High-review-volume group** — storefronts with ≥10 reviews: **us, gb, ca, au, de, ph**. A review-volume proxy, not a download ranking.

**Reading, with the US dominating both groups:**

- **The paywall and ad complaints are a large-market pattern.** High-spend: paywall 52.4% vs 34.7%; ad mismatch 24.7% vs 10.2%. High-volume: paywall 53.1% vs 37.4%; ad mismatch 26.6% vs 9.7%.
- **Billing harm is not.** Unwanted charges are 19.4% vs 17.8% (high-spend) and 18.8% vs 19.4% (high-volume); scam language 21.2% vs 22.0%. **Web checkout is more common in the smaller storefronts** (6.5% vs 2.7%).

## 6.8 Corpus versus public ratings

**Corpus 1.714★ vs 4.355★ across 20,597 public ratings (+2.641★).** The gap is at least +2.3★ in every storefront with ten or more reviews (US +2.70★, UK +2.33★, Canada +2.49★, Australia +2.72★, Germany +2.77★, Philippines +2.69★). **Two structural reasons in the corpus could inflate the public rating:** ratings requested before content unlocks (§3.5), and five-star texts written before use (§4.7). Neither can be measured from reviews.


---

# PART 7 — TIME-TREND ANALYSIS

## 7.1 Method

Reviews have no `version` field, the listing shows no dated release history, and no archived copy of the listing exists (§2.5, §9.E). Eras are therefore defined by **what the corpus itself dates**:

- **E1 — Launch wave** (2020-11-25 → 2021-01-31): the first ten weeks after release, under the name HabitBox.
- **E2 — In-app paywall, spring 2021** (2021-02-01 → 2021-05-31): same model, second volume peak in April.
- **E3 — Web checkout and trial billing** (2021-06-01 → 2021-12-31): starts three days before the first web-checkout review (2021-06-03), after volume fell to 6 reviews in May.
- **E4 — Tail** (2022-01-01 → 2025-06-14): 9 reviews in three and a half years.

## 7.2 The launch wave

- **Volume.** 84.71 reviews per 30 days in E1; 50.25 in E2; 12.34 in E3; 0.21 in E4. The busiest days are 2021-01-03 and 2021-01-04 (15 reviews each), 2020-12-28 and 2021-04-05 (11 each) (§9.H).
- **The launch wave was the least negative of the three large eras** — mean 1.93★ in E1, then 1.55★ in E2 — and the most voted: 72 of 192 E1 reviews (37.5%) carry helpful votes, against 9 of 88 (10.2%) in E3.
- **What E1 reviewers wrote about** was the wall, the ad and the content: `U:pre_use_wall` 55 of 192 (28.6%), `U:ad_mismatch` 52 of 192 (27.1%), `U:value_content` 55 of 192 (28.6%).

## 7.4 What stopped being reported

The in-app dark patterns — **the countdown offer, the review gate, in-app ads, nagging upsells** — and the **spelling errors** all disappear from the corpus by May 2021. Volume also collapses then (92 reviews in April, 6 in May), so **absence after May 2021 is weak evidence** that they were removed.

## 7.5 What got worse

**Money leaving accounts without consent.** In E3, 42 of 88 reviews (47.7%) describe an unwanted charge and 35 of 88 (39.8%) were charged without choosing a purchase; 22 of 88 (25.0%) could not cancel; 31 of 88 (35.2%) report a support failure. The complaints describe sign-ups from Instagram or Facebook to a website, trials billed within days, charges by card or PayPal under another company's name, and no cancellation route in the App Store (§0.5, §3.3).

- `7627531648` (#429, 2021-07-28, de, 1★) — "Sicherheitshalber nochmal im Apple store bei denAbonnements geguckt - da tauchte es nicht auf. Dennoch einen Abbuchung über 30 € via Paypal." *[checked Apple's subscriptions to be safe — it wasn't there; still a 30 € PayPal debit]*
- `7678142732` (#438, 2021-08-10, fr, 1★) — "the only way to cancel your subscription is through mail"
- `7932137213` (#470, 2021-10-19, us, 1★) — "They have duplicate billed me thre months in a row"

## 7.6 The tail, 2022–2025 (9 reviews)

Charges continuing after uninstall (`10915525680`, 2024-02-08: "the payment still running in my credit card"), a subscription that cannot be cancelled after months (`8776444875`, 2022-06-15), a lifetime purchase whose app now freezes (`9025560780`, 2022-08-28: "the app has just an freeze screen"), refund "hoops" (`8243381884`, 2022-01-15), and two unexplained five-star reviews (`8207471494`, 2022-01-05; `12774788552`, 2025-06-14). **Nine reviews cannot describe the app after 2021.**

## 7.7 Names and prices over time

- **HabitBox → GetHabit.** Seven reviews name "HabitBox" between 2020-11-25 and 2021-04-15; none later. The 2022 terms call the app "GetHabit: Motivation & Planner" [external]. The rename date is unknown.
- **In-app prices quoted in E1–E2:** $7.99 a week, $9.99 a month, $39.99 lifetime and $19.99 on offer, a $0.49 trial week, $9.99 PDF packs; the pound and euro equivalents match (£7.99, £38.99, £19.99, 21,99 €). These match the in-app purchase list captured in 2026 ($7.99, $9.99, $19.99, $29.99, $39.99; printable items $9.99).
- **Web-checkout amounts quoted in E3:** $31.96 / 31,98€ / 31.69 USD / 32 USD, £23, 28–30 €, $37, and verification charges of $1, $0.99, 0.96$ and 0,01 € (§9.G).

---

# PART 8 — PRODUCT IMPLICATIONS

Every recommendation is tied to findings by section number and labelled **Immediate fix**, **Experiment** or **Research question**. Because this corpus describes 2020–2021, each item should first be checked against the current build. For a team building in this category, the same items read as **patterns to avoid**.

## 8.1 Immediate fix — one purchase per decision; never stack an offer on a purchase (§0.2, §3.3, §5.5)

**Evidence:** `BILL_DOUBLE_CHARGE` = 26 (5.31%), 13 of them describing the half-price offer shown during or after buying; `PAY_COUNTDOWN_OFFER` = 46 (9.39%); `BILL_WRONG_PLAN` = 15 (3.06%); among the 182 buyers, 24 (13.2%) report a double charge.

**Recommendation:** if a discounted plan is shown after a purchase, it must replace and refund the first, visibly. Remove timers from purchase screens. Show the plan and renewal period on the confirmation screen and in settings.

**Why first:** it converts buyers into refund requests, bank disputes and one-star reviews (§5.5), and a support reply quoted in the corpus acknowledged "a huge number of such mistakes" (`7932137213`).

## 8.2 Immediate fix — let people see the product before paying, and make the listing say what it costs (§0.3, §3.2, §5.6)

**Evidence:** `U:pre_use_wall` = 132 (26.94%), 97 of whom did not pay; `PAY_FREE_LABEL_MISLEADING` = 33 (6.73%); `PAY_CONDITIONAL_WILLING` = 8 (1.63%) name a real trial as their condition; the current description says "free to use" and lists prices that differ from the in-app purchase list (§2.2).

**Recommendation:** a usable free core (a few habits and one challenge) before any purchase screen; a free trial without card details, or a paid trial clearly labelled as paid; description prices that match the in-app purchase list.

## 8.3 Immediate fix — one billing channel, cancellable where it was bought (§0.5, §3.3, §7.5)

**Evidence:** in June–December 2021, `U:unwanted_charge` 42 of 88 (47.7%); `BILL_WEB_CHECKOUT` = 19 (3.88%); `BILL_CANT_CANCEL` = 34 (6.94%); `BILL_CHARGED_AFTER_CANCEL` = 21 (4.29%); `BILL_OTHER_MERCHANT_NAME` = 4 (0.82%); `BILL_TRIAL_SHORTER` = 8 (1.63%); 8 web buyers could not get their purchase into the app.

**Recommendation:** if web checkout is used, link it to an account that unlocks the app, bill under the app's own name, put a self-serve cancel link in every receipt, and make ad trial terms identical to checkout terms.

## 8.4 Immediate fix — reachable support and a refund path (§0.6, §3.8)

**Evidence:** `SUPPORT_UNRESPONSIVE` = 59 (12.04%); `BILL_REFUND_REQUEST` = 64 (13.06%) against `BILL_REFUND_RECEIVED` = 4 (0.82%); 61 of the 227 money-taken reviewers (26.9%) report a support failure; the two reviewers refunded quickly changed their tone (`7879858284` 2★, `8036849766` 3★ "que ladrones no son" *[they're not thieves]*).

**Recommendation:** an in-app contact route that works, a response-time target, and a no-questions refund for double charges and for charges after cancellation.

## 8.5 Immediate fix — remove any link between rating the app and unlocking content (§0.7, §3.5, §4.7)

**Evidence:** `REVIEW_GATE` = 13 (2.65%), 8 of them before using the app; five-star reviews carry more helpful votes than one-star reviews (2.74 vs 0.37 on average); corpus 1.714★ against 4.355★ public.

**Recommendation:** ask for ratings only after a completed habit streak, never as a condition. **Research question:** check the store's rules on rating requests against the 2020–2021 flow.

## 8.6 Experiment — advertise the app that exists, or build what the ads show (§0.4, §3.5)

**Evidence:** `ADS_MISLEADING` = 72 (14.69%), 52 of them buyers; `USE_CLEANING` = 12 (2.45%) wanted the advertised cleaning checklist.

**Experiment:** run creatives that show real screens against the current ones and measure refund requests and one-star reviews per purchase, not only installs. Alternatively, ship the cleaning and personality-based content the ads promised.

## 8.7 Experiment — put the content inside the plan (§3.2, §3.6)

**Evidence:** `PAY_PREMIUM_INCOMPLETE` = 38 (7.76%), 30 of them buyers; `CONTENT_THIN` = 54 (11.02%); the listing still sells "Access to all printable items" separately.

**Experiment:** include printables in any lifetime or yearly plan and measure refund requests among buyers.

## 8.8 Immediate fix — the habit loop itself (§3.6, §3.7)

**Evidence:** `DEF_REMINDERS` = 18 (3.67%); `DEF_CANT_TRACK` = 7 (1.43%); `DEF_BUG_UI` = 28 (5.71%); `FUNC_LIMITED` = 13 (2.65%) — cannot rename, reorder or count habits.

**Recommendation:** reminders that fire at the set time, marking any habit done, editable habits and count-based habits. These are table stakes in the category.

## 8.9 Research questions

- **Is the rating base comparable to the reviews?** The public rating is 2026-cumulative; the reviews are 2020–2021 and partly gate-prompted.
- **What does the current build do?** The last update is 2024-03-26; nothing in the corpus describes it.
- **What is "PersonalityPath"** to this developer, and what did the 2021 web checkout promise?
- **How many double charges and charges after cancellation were refunded?**

## 8.10 What this corpus says *not* to copy or prioritise

| Don't | Why |
|---|---|
| Put a purchase screen before any use | 132 pre-use-wall reviews; 97 never paid (§3.2) |
| Stack a timed discount on a purchase | 26 double charges, half tied to the offer (§3.3) |
| Bill outside the store without account linking and a cancel link | E3: 47.7% unwanted charges (§7.5) |
| Read 4.355★ as satisfaction | Rate-to-unlock and reversed vote pattern (§0.7) |
| Prioritise new features before fixing billing | `U:request_any` = 17 (3.47%); most reviewers never reached the product (§3.11) |


---

# PART 9 — EVIDENCE APPENDIX

## 9.A Counting rules

1. **Denominator** is 490 (every review in `reviews.jsonl`) unless the sentence names a narrower one: a star band, an era, the US (203), the UK (52), the rest of the corpus, a market group, the 182 who chose to pay, the 45 charged without choosing, or the 227 money-taken reviewers. Segment rates are written "n of N".
2. **Codes are non-exclusive** (mean 4.85 per review including mechanical codes). **Unions count each review once.**
3. **Mean ★** for a code is the mean rating of the reviews carrying it.
4. **Signal band** is applied to the share of the stated denominator (§1.5); Wilson 95% intervals throughout.
5. **Mechanical codes** — `META_LOW_INFO` (HTML-unescaped, stripped body under 60 characters), `META_EDITED` (`is_edited` true), `META_HAS_VOTES` (`vote_count` > 0), `META_HABITBOX_NAME` ("habit box" or "habitbox" in title or body) — are assigned by the build from the source record, never by hand.
6. **`PAID_EXPLICIT`** requires the reviewer to say they bought, subscribed, paid (including a paid trial week), or to ask for their money back or call the purchase a waste of a named sum. **`CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN`** is used instead when money was taken and the reviewer does not say they chose a purchase. The two never co-occur (check C5).
7. **`U:unwanted_charge`** covers double charges, wrong plans, price mismatches, early trial billing, shorter-than-advertised trials, charges after cancelling and unauthorised charges. It does not include a charge the reviewer chose and merely regrets.
8. **`SCAM_LANGUAGE`** requires a fraud or deception word ("scam", "fraud", "con", "crooks", "bait and switch", "shady", "steal", "Abzocke", "truffa", "estafa", "timo", "robo", "arnaque", "мошенники" and similar). "Rip off" alone is not counted.
9. **`PRAISE_GENERIC`** is assigned when a review approves but names no product quality; it never co-occurs with specific praise (check C1).
10. **Eras** are E1 2020-11-25 → 2021-01-31, E2 2021-02-01 → 2021-05-31, E3 2021-06-01 → 2021-12-31, E4 2022-01-01 → 2025-06-14.
11. **Language** is hand-assigned and checked against Cyrillic and Czech script detection and an English function-word test (checks L1–L3).
12. **No deduplication** was performed; none was needed (§1.3).
13. **Prices** are recorded as written by the reviewer, in the reviewer's currency (§9.G).

## 9.B Taxonomy — all 90 codes

| Code | Dir | Family | n | Definition |
|---|:-:|---|---:|---|
| `FREE_TIER_OK` | + | Paywall | 5 | Says they used the app, or parts of it, without paying. |
| `PAY_CONDITIONAL_WILLING` | ~ | Paywall | 8 | Would pay, or would have paid, under a condition: a real trial, a lower price, sync, a working app, a preview. |
| `PAY_COUNTDOWN_OFFER` | - | Paywall | 46 | A discounted (usually half-price) lifetime offer, often with a countdown or time limit, shown before or right after another purchase. |
| `PAY_FREE_LABEL_MISLEADING` | - | Paywall | 33 | Says the store 'Free' label, the listing's 'free to use' wording, or an ad presented the app as free when it is not. |
| `PAY_HARD_WALL` | - | Paywall | 96 | Cannot use or even see the app without paying: a purchase/subscription screen at onboarding, every tap leads to payment, or the free version does nothing. |
| `PAY_NO_FREE_TRIAL` | - | Paywall | 58 | Says there is no (free) trial, no preview or demo, cannot try before buying, or the trial offered is too short to judge. |
| `PAY_PAID_TRIAL` | o | Paywall | 29 | The 'trial' costs money ($0.49, $0.99, €1, a $1 verification) or needs payment details before use. |
| `PAY_PREMIUM_INCOMPLETE` | - | Paywall | 38 | After paying (trial, subscription or 'lifetime'), content is still locked or extra purchases are needed (PDFs, checklists, challenges). |

| `PAY_PRICE_TOO_HIGH` | - | Paywall | 54 | Price too high, overpriced, not worth the money for what the app is. |

| `PAY_WEEKLY_PLAN` | - | Paywall | 20 | Mentions the weekly plan or a weekly charge (e.g. '$8 per week', '69 kroners per WEEK', 'charged weekly'). |
| `PAY_WISH_FREE` | ~ | Paywall | 6 | Wishes it were free / asks 'why pay', without a harder objection. |

| `BILL_CANT_CANCEL` | - | Billing | 34 | No way to cancel; the subscription is not in Apple's subscription list; cancel instructions or link do not work. |

## 9.C Validation, and what it caught

`88-validate.py` runs **64 checks, all passing** (output in `Temp/88-validate-out.txt`; log in `Temp/88-validation-log.md`): 10 structural (every ID mapped, zero unknown IDs, zero intra-theme duplicates, zero unassigned records, every code used, map consistency), 4 re-deriving the mechanical codes, 3 on language, **36 tying codes to literal review text** in the corpus's languages (prices, fraud words, weekly plans, countdown offers, the review gate, in-app ads, social platforms, merchant names, web checkout, language, the "free" label, double charges, purchase and charge vocabulary, refunds, cancellation, reminders, spelling, churn, warnings, trial lengths, payment details, restore, paid trials, escalation, named competitors, cleaning, mental-health uses, subscription objections, review distrust and review updates), and 11 consistency rules.

**The first run failed 12 of 64 checks. Two were real classification errors, fixed in the hand file:**

| Review / code | Error | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `6726845020` (#3) | "Thanks for taking money and failing to produce" was coded as a chosen purchase with regret | Changed to `CHARGED_NOT_CHOSEN`; `PAID_REGRET` removed |
| `7254145251` (#374) | "49 cents to even look at the app" was coded as a purchase | `PAID_EXPLICIT` removed |

**The other failures were defects in the checks**, each fixed and documented: missing vocabulary ("1/2 price", German "Werbung" and "Abbuchung", Spanish "Pagas" and "subscripción", Italian "prova gratuito", "waste of £20", misspelled "subcription"); an English-word test that first missed short English reviews and then flagged Romance words; and two text-to-code checks that needed documented exemptions (reminder mentions that are free substitutes or upsell pop-ups; "clean life" is not a cleaning use).

**Documented exemptions in the final run:** T7a — `7158167334` ("reviews on Facebook"), `7502946196` (contacted support on Twitter and Instagram); T11 — `7044839098` (listing claim without the word "free"); T12 — `6762344201` (£30 = £9.99 + £20); T17b — nine reminder mentions that are not defects; T27 — `6866515956`, `7254605128`, `7178683778`; C3 — `7705118821` (3★ "It is ok and ye"); C7 — `6934056192` (in-app prices double the listing; no charge).

**Known residual error risk.** `PAID_EXPLICIT` rests on the reviewer's wording; refund requests without a stated purchase are counted as purchase evidence (rule 6), which may place a few unauthorised-charge complaints on the "chose to pay" side. The billing codes overlap by design — a charge after cancelling a trial can be both `BILL_TRIAL_CONVERSION` and `BILL_CHARGED_AFTER_CANCEL` — and no headline depends on any one of them; `U:unwanted_charge` counts each review once. `ACQ_SOCIAL_AD` includes reviews that say only "the ad".

## 9.D Sensitivity

| Measure | Full (490) | Without the reviews under 60 chars (408) | Without the US (287) | Without reviews carrying helpful votes (358) | E1–E2 only (to 2021-05-31) (393) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Mean rating | 1.714 | 1.451 | 1.763 | 1.561 | 1.738 |
| `U:paywall_any` | 48.2% | 53.4% | 42.5% | 47.5% | 56.7% |
| `U:pre_use_wall` | 26.9% | 29.9% | 23.3% | 26.3% | 31.8% |
| `U:money_taken` | 46.3% | 53.2% | 42.5% | 47.2% | 40.2% |
| `U:unwanted_charge` | 19.0% | 22.5% | 20.6% | 20.4% | 12.5% |
| `BILL_DOUBLE_CHARGE` | 5.3% | 6.1% | 4.2% | 4.5% | 5.9% |
| `U:ad_mismatch` | 21.2% | 24.8% | 15.3% | 18.7% | 25.2% |
| `SCAM_LANGUAGE` | 21.4% | 25.2% | 20.9% | 21.5% | 16.8% |
| `U:support_negative` | 14.3% | 17.2% | 14.6% | 15.9% | 9.4% |
| `U:praise_any` | 18.4% | 13.2% | 19.5% | 13.7% | 19.6% |
| `U:praise_specific` | 6.7% | 6.6% | 7.0% | 5.0% | 7.4% |
| `U:defect_any` | 16.9% | 19.1% | 17.8% | 19.0% | 17.0% |
| `PRAISE_GENERIC` | 8.2% | 2.9% | 7.7% | 5.3% | 8.4% |

**Dropping the 82 reviews under 60 characters raises every negative share** (`U:paywall_any` 48.2% → 53.4%) and lowers the mean to 1.451★. **Dropping the US** lowers the paywall share to 42.5% and the ad-mismatch share from 21.2% to 15.3%, and raises the mean to 1.763★. **Dropping the 132 reviews with helpful votes** lowers praise from 18.4% to 13.7% and the mean to 1.561★. **Restricting to E1–E2** raises the paywall share to 56.7% and lowers unwanted charges to 12.5%. No headline in Part 0 changes direction under any of the four.

## 9.F Absence tests — 20 patterns, every hit read

Run by `88-aggregate.py` §M; every hit is printed in `Temp/88-aggregate-out.txt` and was read individually.

| Claim tested | Keyword hits | After reading | Result |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Crash / freeze | 11 | 1 crash, 8 freezes, stuck or blank screens; 2 are "stuck with" a purchase | **Rare**; coded `DEF_BUG_UI` |
| Widget | 1 | "Vidgets does not work" | One broken widget; no praise |
| Sync / account / backup | 28 | 9 describe no account, login or sync; the rest are bank accounts | Coded `NO_ACCOUNT_SYNC` |
| Apple Watch | 5 | all the verb "watch" | **Absent** |
| Apple Health | 1 | wants progress saved to Apple Health | One request |
| Streaks (feature) | 2 | a competitor app; "log a streak" | **No streak feature discussed** |
| Dark mode / theme | 3 | 1 praise, 1 dislikes the dark background, 1 likes the colours | Coded |
| iPad / Mac / desktop | 1 | wants an iPad layout | Coded `REQ_IPAD` |
| Android / Google Play | 1 | advice to pay only in the iOS or Play store | **No platform comparison** |
| Privacy / data sharing | 3 | 2 object to giving card data; 1 false hit | **No tracking concern** |
| In-app AI / coach | 2 | both false hits (French "j'y ai", Italian "ai tuoi") | **Absent** |
| Accessibility | 2 | 1 wants smaller text on iPad; 1 false hit | One mention |
| Battery | 1 | false hit ("down the drain") | **Absent** |
| Family Sharing | 0 | — | **Absent** |
| Journal / mood | 4 | a journal as a substitute or a description | **No mood feature discussed** |
| Meditation | 2 | 1 blank meditation page; 1 content description | Coded |
| Sleep | 1 | false hit ("went to sleep") | **Absent** |
| Water | 3 | 1 onboarding question, 2 water habits | Coded |
| Developer responded to a review | 23 | 22 report no response; 1 saw a response months later (`8034491173`) | **One reported response** |
| Refund received | 6 | 4 refunds received; 2 false hits | Coded `BILL_REFUND_RECEIVED` |

## 9.H Burst, solicitation and authenticity checks

- **No exact-duplicate text, no repeat authors, no duplicate IDs** (§1.3).
- **Busiest days: 15 reviews each on 2021-01-03 and 2021-01-04**; 11 each on 2020-12-28 and 2021-04-05. They are negative days (9 one-star reviews on each of the two January days; mean 1.87★ and 2.13★) spread across 5+ storefronts — **an event response, not a positive burst.**
- **Repeated titles:** "Scam" ×12, "Not free" ×7, "Rip off" ×5, "Don’t do it" ×5, "Abzocke" ×4 — independent complaints with different bodies.
- **Helpful votes favour short five-star reviews.** 132 reviews carry votes: 27 five-star, 5 four-star, 6 three-star, 14 two-star, 80 one-star. Mean votes per review by band: 2.74 / 1.77 / 0.44 / 1.29 / 0.37. **11 five-star US reviews under 60 characters carry five or more votes** — `6783534274`, `6802040366`, `6808378171`, `6822135295`, `6829241270`, `6840329100`, `6866515956`, `6909455508`, `7057150221`, `7082049580`, `7190296502` — including "Cool" (9 votes) and "Great app" (12 votes). Long, specific one-star reviews rarely exceed five votes (6 of 355).
- **Rate-to-unlock:** 13 reviews (§3.5); 23 reviews say they have not used the app yet.
- **Allegations in reviews:** fake positive reviews (`6794221347`, `6936787613`, `6907737679`), paid reviews (`7029621975`), a deleted review (`7441400045`).
- **Author names:** two five-star reviews carry Cyrillic-script author names on non-Cyrillic storefronts' English text (`6759949449` ua, `6772325432` us, 7 votes). Recorded, not evidence of anything.
- **Edited:** 4. **Rating–text contradictions:** 3 (§4.7).

**Reading.** Short, generic five-star texts attracting more helpful votes than detailed complaints, in the same weeks reviewers describe being made to rate the app, is **consistent with solicited or coordinated ratings. It is not proof**, and the corpus cannot show who voted.

No developer responses were extractable from the captured listing [external]. **Support replies quoted by reviewers:**

| `7084713878` (#256) | "they told me we don’t do refunds and that they do offer checklists" |
| `8034491173` (#476) | "I may have accidentally selected the weekly subscription and that there is no way for them to verify that" |
| `7886293592` (#465) | "dicen que están colapsados debido a que tienen alta demanda" *[they say they are overwhelmed by high demand]* |
| `7887870313` (#467) | "they told me that my Apple ID email was unable to be found" |
